213: ## 3. WS-2: Standardize & Extend the Canonical Event Model
214: 
215: ### 3.1 Task-level vs project-level events
216: 
217: `events.task_id` is currently NOT NULL. Recommendation: make it nullable in migration
218: `002_unified_events.sql` so project-level agent lifecycle events (session started with
219: no task context, project summary refresh) can be recorded. Events with `task_id = NULL`
220: appear in project-level history but not in per-task timelines. An index
221: `(project_id, task_id, sequence)` supports both query shapes.
222: 
223: ### 3.2 Categories & new event types
224: 
225: | Category | Types (new marked *) | Notes |
226: |----------|----------------------|-------|
227: | task | task_created, task_updated, task_claimed, task_released, task_transitioned | existing |
228: | agent | agent_session_started*, agent_session_ended*, agent_interrupted*, agent_timed_out* | only where reliably observable |
229: | progress | progress_milestone*, progress_fulfilment* | fulfilment triggers checkpoint *evaluation* |
230: | decision | decision_recorded, decision_superseded | existing |
231: | verification | verification_passed*, verification_failed*, verification_skipped* | |
232: | recovery | checkpoint_recorded (existing), recovery_started*, recovery_completed* | |
233: 
234: Add `category` column (default `task`) and `source` column to `events`; index
235: `(task_id, category, sequence)`. Existing rows default to category `task`, source `core`.
236: 
237: ### 3.3 Payload schemas (shared/types/events.ts)
238: 
239: Every new type gets a strict Zod payload schema. Agent events carry `agent_id`,
240: `session_id` where applicable, and bounded free-text fields (summary, findings,
241: blockers, next_steps, files_changed). Verification events carry `check_type` and
242: outcome detail. No schema allows an agent event to set `status` or mutate task state.
243: 
244: ### 3.4 EventService extensions
245: 
246: - `recordAgentEvent`, `recordProgressEvent`, `recordVerificationEvent`,
247:   `recordRecoveryEvent` — all funnel through one internal `append()` that enforces
248:   sequence assignment, payload validation, idempotency, and per-task/project scope.
249: - Idempotent ingestion: an agent may resend an event with the same idempotency key;
250:   duplicate content with a new key is stored (append-only) but flagged in payload
251:   hash for policy dedup (see §5.3).
252: 
253: ### 3.5 New MCP tools (adapter)
254: 
255: | Tool | Purpose |
256: |------|---------|
257: | start_agent_session / end_agent_session | record agent lifecycle |
258: | report_interruption / report_timeout | record interruption/timeout |
259: | report_progress_milestone | record a milestone |
260: | report_fulfilment | record fulfilment AND trigger checkpoint evaluation |
261: | report_verification | record pass/fail/skip |
262: | evaluate_checkpoint | run policy; create checkpoint only if warranted |
263: 
264: All require idempotency key + agent_id; none performs an authoritative transition.
265: 
266: **Acceptance**: new event types persist, validate, appear in task/project history with
267: source+category; idempotent ingestion proven; no agent event changes task status.
268: 
269: ---
270: 
271: ## 4. WS-3: Unified History in APIs & Dashboard
272: 
273: - `GET /api/events` accepts `category`, `source`, `task_id`/`project_id` filters and
274:   returns the enriched envelope. `EventService.listEvents` gains the same filters.
275: - Dashboard `TaskDetailPage` renders a unified chronological timeline: category chip,
276:   source badge (`core` vs agent id), authoritative vs claimed distinction (task
277:   transition rows visually distinct from agent claims), payload detail on expand.
278: - Project detail page gains a project-level history section for task-less events.
279: 
280: **Acceptance**: dashboard shows one merged timeline; filters work; existing tests pass.
281: 
282: ---
283: 
284: ## 5. WS-4: Checkpoint Schema & Provenance
285: 
286: ### 5.1 Migration `003_checkpoint_provenance.sql` (project DB)
287: 
288: ```sql
289: ALTER TABLE checkpoints ADD COLUMN creation_mode TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'manual'; -- manual | auto
290: ALTER TABLE checkpoints ADD COLUMN trigger_event_id TEXT REFERENCES events(id);
291: ALTER TABLE checkpoints ADD COLUMN previous_checkpoint_id TEXT REFERENCES checkpoints(id);
292: 
293: CREATE TABLE checkpoint_sources (
294:   checkpoint_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES checkpoints(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
295:   event_id      TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES events(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
296:   role          TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'evidence',  -- evidence | trigger | baseline
297:   PRIMARY KEY (checkpoint_id, event_id, role)
298: );
299: CREATE INDEX idx_checkpoint_sources_event ON checkpoint_sources(event_id);
300: ```
301: 
302: ### 5.2 Trade-off: relational join table vs JSON array
303: 
304: | | Relational `checkpoint_sources` | JSON `source_event_ids` |
305: |---|---|---|
306: | Integrity | FK to append-only events; orphan-proof | none |
307: | Query "which checkpoints cite event X" | indexed join | JSON scan |
308: | Write cost | extra rows in same tx | one column |
309: | Convention fit | matches relational schema style | `decisions.supporting_event_ids` precedent |
310: 
311: Recommendation: relational table. `decisions.supporting_event_ids` remains as-is (no
312: retro-fit required) but new decision writes may also populate a `decision_sources`
313: table later; out of scope here.
314: 
315: ### 5.3 CheckpointService changes
316: 
317: `createCheckpoint` accepts optional `creation_mode`, `trigger_event_id`,
318: `previous_checkpoint_id`, `source_event_ids[]`; in ONE transaction inserts the
319: checkpoint, inserts `checkpoint_sources` rows, updates `tasks.latest_checkpoint_id`,
320: and appends a `checkpoint_recorded` event. Idempotency key dedup prevents double
321: creation from retries. `getCheckpoint(checkpointId)` returns provenance.
322: 
323: Concurrency: the transaction re-reads `tasks.version` and refuses to describe a newer
324: version than the evidence supports (expected-version check).
325:

332: Pure function: `evaluate(task, latestCheckpoint, eventsSinceCheckpoint, trigger) →
333: { warranted, reason, suggestedContent, sourceEventIds }`.

import type { FastifyInstance } from 'fastify';
import { listEventsQuerySchema, ListEventsQuery } from '@workjournal/shared';
import { validateQuery } from '../hooks/validation.js';
import { buildServices } from '../services.js';

export async function registerEventRoutes(server: FastifyInstance) {
  server.get('/', { preHandler: [validateQuery(listEventsQuerySchema)] }, async (request) => {
    const query = request.query as ListEventsQuery;
    const scope = server.projectDbs.forTask(query.task_id);
    return buildServices(scope.db).events.listEvents(query.task_id, query.limit, query.offset);
  });
}

+
+## D-016 — Preserve the Core/adapter split; MCP stays in the adapter (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** The MCP adapter registers tools, resources and prompts; resource and prompt handlers call Core REST APIs. There is no MCP-in-core move. A single-process layout is documented as an alternative in plan §9.4 but is not a prerequisite.
+
+**Bridge.** Core emits post-commit change notifications over an internal event bus and a new, minimal long-poll/SSE endpoint (`GET /api/changes/stream`). The adapter subscribes and translates Core change records into MCP `notifications/resources/updated`, `notifications/prompts/list_changed`, and `notifications/resources/list_changed` for each connected MCP session. Browser dashboard refresh is a separate mechanism and is not coupled to MCP.
+
+**Reason.** Preserves the "adapter never touches SQLite" isolation and keeps the existing two-process Core/adapter architecture intact while adding change notifications (WS-8).
+
+**Consequences.** Notifications flow Core → adapter → MCP sessions; the dashboard uses its own polling/SSE path; the single-process alternative remains deferred unless the bridge proves unreliable in practice.
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (docs/WorkJournal_post_MVP.md §2, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-017 — Canonical event envelope with source + category (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** Introduce a stable envelope: `event_id`, `occurred_at` (UTC), `source` (`core` | `mcp_tool` | `agent:<agent-id>`), `category`, `type`, `project_id`, `task_id` (nullable), `actor`, `payload` (validated per type), `sequence`. Agent-reported events are recorded as ledger events — they never perform an authoritative task transition. Low-level tool-call logging is opt-in.
+
+**Reason.** Unifies the task and agent ledgers with nullable `task_id` for project-level agent events; `source`/`category` give authority badges for the unified timeline; keeping task transitions inside the single transition function protects the D-001..D-004 invariants (WS-2, WS-5).
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-018 — Checkpoint provenance via relational join table (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** Checkpoint provenance uses a `checkpoint_sources` join table (`checkpoint_id`, `event_id`, `role`) rather than a JSON array of source event ids: referential integrity against append-only events, indexed support for "which checkpoints cite this event", and consistency with existing relational conventions. Trade-off analysis is in plan §5.2; `decisions.supporting_event_ids` is left unchanged (no retro-fit).
+
+**Reason.** FK integrity and reverse lookups outweigh the simpler write cost of a JSON column; matches the project-DB relational style.
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §5.2, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-019 — Deterministic checkpoint policy; compaction deferred out of v2 (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** Checkpoint *policy* decides whether a checkpoint is warranted — a pure function over task state, latest checkpoint, and events since. Checkpoint *content* is assembled deterministically from bounded event slices; no model runs in Core. The optional compaction provider layer (Ollama/remote, plan §6.3) is explicitly deferred: the agent produces the fulfilment/progress narrative through ledger events and Core only structures and validates it. The `Compactor` interface remains specified so a provider can be added later, but no provider, configuration, or test surface lands in v2.
+
+**Reason.** Determinism and inspectability are hard requirements; meetb's decision is that v2 relies on the agent itself, with no model in Core (WS-5).
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §6.3, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-020 — Prompts are data in the registry DB, dual-scope with shadowing (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** Prompts are validated templates (message definitions), never executable code, stored as rows in the registry DB with a nullable `project_id` (NULL = global scope). Built-ins are seeded global rows, editable as overrides. A project-scoped prompt with the same name shadows any global prompt or built-in for that project only — global rows are never modified by shadowing and other projects are unaffected. The MCP adapter resolves project scope by matching its working directory against registered project roots (no match → global prompts only) and re-reads the registry on change to re-register dynamically.
+
+**Reason.** Dual-scope storage lets one API/dashboard/MCP sync serve both scopes while keeping project DBs pure per-project state (prompts are not stored in project DBs).
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §8.1, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-021 — Extensible agent-integration registry behind `workjournal setup` (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** Setup logic lives behind an `AgentIntegration` interface (discovery, plan, apply, validate, uninstall, capabilities); the CLI iterates a frozen registry array. The only adapter in this release is OpenCode; adding another agent (e.g. Cline) is a new adapter file plus one registry entry — no changes to the setup command flow. Interactive selection is a zero-dependency `node:readline` numbered multiselect. Config scope is global by default (`~/.config/opencode/opencode.json(c)`) with an explicit `--project` flag for cwd-local config. OpenCode config files are JSONC; merges use Microsoft `jsonc-parser` minimal-edit operations so comments, formatting, and unrelated keys survive; the parser's duplicate-key limitation is documented and reported.
+
+**Reason.** Keeps the setup command closed to modification but open to extension, keeps the CLI dependency-free and SEA-safe, and preserves user config across setup runs.
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §10.2–10.3, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-022 — OpenCode lifecycle plugin: embed + extract, reports to a Core ingestion API (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** The lifecycle plugin is a small self-contained JS module authored in the repo, embedded into the SEA executable at build time (same generated-embed pattern as migrations and dashboard UI) and written to `~/.workjournal/integrations/opencode/plugin/` by `workjournal setup`. It default-exports `{ id, setup(ctx) }`; OpenCode V2 reads `id`/`setup` off the export, so no `@opencode/plugin` runtime dependency is needed in the extracted location (embedded `Plugin.define` shim as fallback). It observes only reliably observable signals (session lifecycle, interruption, idle, errors) via `ctx.event.subscribe()` and optional tool hooks, and POSTs canonical envelopes to Core `POST /api/ingest/events`. Delivery uses a bounded queue in `ctx.storage` with backoff when Core is down; the plugin never autostarts Core. Low-level tool-call logging is opt-in via plugin options, default off; idle/errors/interruption are signals, never proof of task completion.
+
+**Reason.** Embed+extract keeps the plugin self-contained with no runtime dependency; ledger-only ingestion plus the transition-function boundary guarantees plugin events cannot mutate task status (WS-9).
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §10.5, 2026-10-23).
+
+## D-023 — Ingestion API accepts ledger envelopes only; task transitions stay with the transition function (2026-10-23)
+
+**Decision.** `POST /api/ingest/events` accepts a bounded batch (≤ 50) of canonical envelopes restricted to ledger categories (`agent`, `progress`, `verification`, `recovery` signals). Task-lifecycle or transition-shaped payloads are rejected with `400`. Idempotency is by client-generated `event_id` (retry-safe dedup; per-event `accepted` | `duplicate` | `rejected` responses). The single transition function remains the only path that changes task status.
+
+**Reason.** Agent observability events enter the ledger without any path for them to corrupt task state, preserving the D-001..D-004 invariants (WS-9 plugin→Core integration).
+
+**Status.** Approved via post-MVP plan (§2, §10.5, 2026-10-23).
+
+---
+

In `docs/decisions.md` that content is recorded as **D-012** (SEA single executable, 2026-10-19); **D-011** is the `PROJECTS_DIRECTORY`/Docker path-mapping decision. 9: ## 1. Purpose
10: 
11: Pin the canonical event envelope and the WS-2 event-model extensions as a reviewable spec before any schema or service code is written (repo rule: spec before implementation). WS-2 implements `packages/core/src/domain/event-service.ts` extensions and `shared/src/types/events.ts` additions against this contract.
12: 
13: ## 2. Canonical envelope
14: 
15: Every event persisted by Core — task lifecycle or agent ledger — is stored and exchanged as one canonical envelope. Field table:
16: 
17: | Field | Type | Notes |
18: |---|---|---|
19: | `event_id` | text | Client-generated id; idempotency/dedup key for ingestion |
20: | `occurred_at` | text (UTC) | Event occurrence timestamp; all timestamps stored in UTC |
21: | `source` | text | `core` \| `mcp_tool` \| `agent:<agent-id>` — who/what produced the event |
22: | `category` | text | `task` \| `agent` \| `progress` \| `decision` \| `verification` \| `recovery` (default `task` for existing rows) |
23: | `type` | text | One of the event types in §3 |
24: | `project_id` | text | Owning project |
25: | `task_id` | text, NULLABLE | NULL for project-level agent lifecycle events; see §3.1 |
26: | `actor` | text | Actor making the record (agent id or `operator`) |
27: | `payload` | json | Per-type payload, validated by strict Zod schema before insert |
28: | `sequence` | integer | Per-task (or per-project for NULL task_id) monotonic sequence |
29: 
30: Existing `events` rows keep `category = 'task'`, `source = 'core'`; migration `002_unified_events.sql` adds `category` and `source` (project DB) plus index `(project_id, task_id, sequence)` supporting both query shapes (§3.1, §3.2).
31: 
32: ### Constraint — agent events never set status
33: 
34: No agent event payload schema allows setting `task.status` or otherwise mutating task state. The single domain-level transition function (`core/src/domain/transitions.ts`, backend AGENTS.md invariant #2) remains the only code path that changes task status; ingestion is ledger-only (§2/D-023, §9.1 of this spec's sibling M10).
35: 
36: ## 3. Categories and event types (plan §3.2)
37: 
38: | Category | Types (* = new) | Notes |
39: |---|---|---|
40: | task | `task_created`, `task_updated`, `task_claimed`, `task_released`, `task_transitioned` | existing |
41: | agent | `agent_session_started*`, `agent_session_ended*`, `agent_interrupted*`, `agent_timed_out*` | only where reliably observable |
42: | progress | `progress_milestone*`, `progress_fulfilment*` | fulfilment triggers checkpoint *evaluation* (§5) |
43: | decision | `decision_recorded`, `decision_superseded` | existing |
44: | verification | `verification_passed*`, `verification_failed*`, `verification_skipped*` | |
45: | recovery | `checkpoint_recorded` (existing), `recovery_started*`, `recovery_completed*` | |
46: 
47: ## 4. Payload schemas (plan §3.3)
48: 
49: Every new type gets a strict Zod payload schema in `shared/src/types/events.ts`. Agent events carry `agent_id`, `session_id` where applicable, and bounded free-text fields (`summary`, `findings`, `blockers`, `next_steps`, `files_changed`). Verification events carry `check_type` and outcome detail.
50: 
51: ## 5. EventService extensions (plan §3.4)
52: 
53: - `recordAgentEvent`, `recordProgressEvent`, `recordVerificationEvent`, `recordRecoveryEvent` — all funnel through one internal `append()` that enforces sequence assignment, payload validation, idempotency, and per-task/project scope.
54: - Idempotent ingestion: an agent may resend an event with the same idempotency key; duplicate content sent with a new key is stored (append-only) but flagged in its payload hash for policy dedup (§5.3 of this doc's sibling M5; plan §5.3).
55: 
56: ## 6. New MCP tools (plan §3.5)
57: 
58: | Tool | Purpose |
59: |---|---|
60: | `start_agent_session` / `end_agent_session` | record agent lifecycle |
61: | `report_interruption` / `report_timeout` | record interruption/timeout |
62: | `report_progress_milestone` | record a milestone |
63: | `report_fulfilment` | record fulfilment AND trigger checkpoint evaluation |
64: | `report_verification` | record pass/fail/skip |
65: | `evaluate_checkpoint` | run policy; create checkpoint only if warranted |
66: 
67: All require idempotency key + `agent_id`; none performs an authoritative task status transition.
68: 
69: ## 7. Coordination notes (no invention)
70: 
71: Plan §10.11 names `agent_idle` as a WS-5 checkpoint-evaluation trigger signal and §3.1's nullable `task_id` as required for project-level plugin events. Those are WS-2/WS-5 concerns; this spec records them only as cross-references and does not add them to the type table above.
72:

74: 
75: - Every field, category, type, and rule above traces to plan §2–§3; nothing added that the plan does not name.

1: # Spec — M10: Event ingestion API (post-MVP)
2: 
3: Status: proposed
4: Date: 2026-10-23
5: Author: Mimo (agent)
6: Plan: docs/WorkJournal_post_MVP.md §2 (D-023), §10.5 (WS-9 plugin→Core), §10.11
7: Source of truth: this spec captures plan §2/D-023 and §10.5 verbatim where quoted; docs-only, no code.
8: 
9: ## 1. Purpose
10: 
11: Pin the `POST /api/ingest/events` contract so WS-2 (nullable `task_id`, envelope schema) and WS-9 (OpenCode lifecycle plugin delivery) implement one shared, reviewable interface. The plugin translates native agent events into this canonical envelope; Core never lets ingestion mutate task status.
12: 
13: ## 2. Endpoint contract (plan §2/D-023)
14: 
15: `POST /api/ingest/events` accepts a **bounded batch of canonical envelopes** (see M3) restricted to **ledger categories only**: `agent`, `progress`, `verification`, `recovery` signals.
16: 
17: - **Batch bound:** ≤ 50 envelopes per request.
18: - **Restriction:** task-lifecycle or transition-shaped payloads are rejected with `400`. The single transition function remains the only path that changes task status (backend AGENTS.md invariant #2).
19: - **Idempotency:** by client-generated `event_id` — retry-safe dedup; resending an accepted `event_id` is a no-op.
20: - **Per-event response:** each envelope in the batch reports one of `accepted` \| `duplicate` \| `rejected`, so a partial batch never silently drops events.
21: 
22: ## 3. Session → task binding (plan §2/D-023)
23: 
24: The MCP adapter forwards OpenCode's `ai.opencode/sessionID` tool-call `_meta` to Core. Core maintains a session↔task binding, established on successful claim-like tools, so later plugin events for the same session are backfilled with `task_id`. Events with no binding remain project-level (`task_id` NULL per plan §3.1/M3).
25: 
26: ## 4. Plugin → Core behavior (plan §10.5 / §10.11)
27: 
28: - The OpenCode lifecycle plugin POSTs canonical envelopes to `POST /api/ingest/events`.
29: - Delivery uses a bounded queue in `ctx.storage` with backoff when Core is down; the plugin never autostarts Core (the MCP adapter's D-013 autostart covers agent work sessions).
30: - Envelopes are restricted to ledger categories — they cannot mutate task state even if the endpoint is abused (loopback-only posture: Zod validation + payload limits, same as all Core routes).
31: - Ingestion envelopes reuse the canonical schema (M3); nullable `task_id` (§3.1) is required for project-level plugin events (plan §10.11).
32: 
33: ## 5. Verification (docs-only)
34: 
35: - Endpoint, batch bound (≤50), ledger-only restriction, `event_id` dedup, and per-event `accepted`/`duplicate`/`rejected` responses all trace to plan §2/D-023 and §10.5.
36: - No task-status mutation through ingestion: consistent with M3 §2 and plan §2/D-023.
37: - git diff for this session contains `docs/specs/*` only.

import { v4 as uuid } from 'uuid';
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { Database } from '../db/connection.js';
import { isEventType, parseEventPayload } from '@workjournal/shared';
import { toEventRow } from './serialize.js';

export interface RecordEventInput {
  projectId: string;
  taskId: string;
  actor: string;
  eventType: string;
  payload: unknown;
}

export interface RecordedEvent {
  id: string;
  sequence: number;
  created_at: string;
}

const objectPayload = z.record(z.unknown());

/**
 * Payloads of the defined event types are validated against their schema; any other
 * event type (free-form, recorded by an agent or the dashboard) must at least be a
 * JSON object. Validation happens before the insert, inside the caller's transaction.
 */
function validatePayload(eventType: string, payload: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
  if (isEventType(eventType)) {
    return parseEventPayload(eventType, payload) as Record<string, unknown>;
  }
  return objectPayload.parse(payload ?? {});
}

/**
 * Append-only per-task history (scope A9). Never called inside its own transaction:
 * the caller (task/checkpoint/decision service) owns the transaction so the state change
 * and its event commit together.
 */
export class EventService {
  constructor(private db: Database) {}

  recordEvent(data: RecordEventInput): RecordedEvent {
    const payload = validatePayload(data.eventType, data.payload);
    const id = uuid();
    const now = new Date().toISOString();

    const lastSeq = this.db.raw
      .prepare('SELECT MAX(sequence) as max_seq FROM events WHERE task_id = ?')
      .get(data.taskId) as any;
    const sequence = (lastSeq?.max_seq || 0) + 1;

    this.db.raw
      .prepare(
        `INSERT INTO events (id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, payload, created_at)
         VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,
      )
      .run(
        id,
        data.projectId,
        data.taskId,
        sequence,
        data.actor,
        data.eventType,
        JSON.stringify(payload),
        now,
      );

    return { id, sequence, created_at: now };
  }

  listEvents(taskId: string, limit = 100, offset = 0) {
    return (this.db.raw
      .prepare('SELECT * FROM events WHERE task_id = ? ORDER BY sequence ASC LIMIT ? OFFSET ?')
      .all(taskId, limit, offset) as any[]).map(toEventRow);
  }

  getEventsSince(taskId: string, sinceSequence: number) {
    return (this.db.raw
      .prepare('SELECT * FROM events WHERE task_id = ? AND sequence > ? ORDER BY sequence ASC')

## Review: current state vs WS-2/WS-3

**Commit `101d0fd`** (pushed as `origin/master`; `origin/main`/local base is clean at `eabdefe`) mixed three things:

1. **Corrupted/broken code** — `mcp-adapter/src/client.ts` was truncated to 72 bytes of binary garbage; the Cline plugin (`backend/cline-plugin-workjournal/`, 455 lines) posts to the wrong URL (`/api/events` not `/api/ingest/events`), references an out-of-scope `ctx` (runtime `ReferenceError`), sends event types the backend rejects (`agent_tool_call`/`agent_tool_result` don't exist), and wears an invented "WS-3" label (WS-3 is the dashboard/history workstream — the event model is WS-2).
2. **Legitimate but unfinished WS-2 groundwork** — migration 002, shared event types, ingest route, EventService rewrite — directionally per spec M3 but never made to compile/pass (your own task DB marks WS-2 "completed" while typecheck/tests were broken — that status is false).
3. **Docs that define the actual task** — the approved plan `docs/WorkJournal_post_MVP.md`, decisions **D-016..D-023** (the envelope, ingestion API, and OpenCode-plugin contracts), and specs M3/M10/M14.

Your task DB (`beea14ae` WS-2, `d74cf6d3` WS-3, plus WS-9.5–9.7 for the OpenCode plugin) matches what you said: restart WS-2 + WS-3, then OpenCode plugin, then Cline plugin (which per M14 §7 is *new scope* — needs a D-024 decision entry when we do it).

## Plan of action

- **Phase 0 — clean slate:** `git reset --hard eabdefe` (kills the commit + all working-tree changes, including the staged client.ts fix and the bogus DB state that falsely marked WS-2 completed), then restore **`docs/` only** from `101d0fd` — the plan, D-016..D-023, and specs *are* the task definition, so deleting them would make "restart WS-2/WS-3 against the task need" impossible. Commit as one fresh docs commit.
- **Phase 1 — WS-2 rework:** migration 002 (nullable `task_id`, `category`/`source`, indexes) + 002b (session↔task binding), shared canonical envelope + strict per-type payload schemas per M3, single `append()` funnel in EventService with `event_id` idempotency, `POST /api/ingest/events` (≤50, ledger-only, per-event accepted/duplicate/rejected), session correlation, agent-reporting MCP tools. Real-SQLite tests.
- **Phase 2 — WS-3:** `GET /api/events` filters + enriched envelope, unified timeline on TaskDetailPage (category chips, source/authority badges), project-level history on ProjectDetailPage.
- **Phase 3 — OpenCode plugin (first priority):** spike the real plugin API (event names verified against current docs, not guessed), then implement per D-022 (`{id, setup(ctx)}`, `ctx.event.subscribe`, bounded queue in `ctx.storage`, backoff, no autostart) and wire it into your OpenCode config so **the events system is actually operational end-to-end today** (plugin → ingestion → project DB → dashboard timeline). The full `workjournal setup` CLI follows.
- **Phase 4 — Cline plugin:** rewrite from scratch against the same ingestion contract, with D-024 logged.

1: import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
2: import { resolve } from 'node:path';
3: import type { MigrationFile } from './migrate.js';
4: import {
5:   embeddedRegistryMigrations,

7: } from './embedded-migrations.generated.js';
8: 
9: /**
10:  * Migration sources that work in every packaging mode (D-011):
11:  *
12:  *  - tsc/tsx runs (dev, Docker, native CLI): the real `.sql` directories sit
13:  *    next to the package, so they are read from disk — source-tree migration
14:  *    edits apply without a rebuild.
15:  *  - the single-file SEA executable: no files exist next to the binary, so the
16:  *    arrays embedded at build time by `scripts/embed-migrations.ts` are used.
17:  *
18:  * Both forms are generated from the same committed `.sql` files, so the
19:  * `__migrations` ledger is identical either way.
20:  */
21: 
22: /** Directory of this module at runtime, or undefined inside a bundle. */
23: function moduleDir(): string | undefined {
24:   return typeof import.meta.dirname === 'string' ? import.meta.dirname : undefined;
25: }
26: 
27: function fromDiskOrEmbedded(
28:   diskDir: string | undefined,
29:   embedded: MigrationFile[],
30:   what: string,
31: ): string | MigrationFile[] {
32:   if (diskDir && existsSync(diskDir)) return diskDir;
33:   if (embedded.length > 0) return embedded;
34:   throw new Error(
35:     `No ${what} found${diskDir ? ` at ${diskDir}` : ' (bundled binary)'} and none embedded in ` +
36:       `this build. Rebuild with \`pnpm build\` (it re-runs the embed step).`,
37:   );
38: }
39: 
40: /** Registry migrations: `<core>/migrations` on disk, else the embedded copy. */
41: export function registryMigrationsSource(): string | MigrationFile[] {
42:   const dir = moduleDir();
43:   return fromDiskOrEmbedded(
44:     dir ? resolve(dir, '../../migrations') : undefined,
45:     embeddedRegistryMigrations(),
46:     'registry migrations',
47:   );
48: }
49: 
50: /** Project migrations: `<core>/project-migrations` on disk, else embedded. */
51: export function projectMigrationsSource(): string | MigrationFile[] {
52:   const dir = moduleDir();
53:   return fromDiskOrEmbedded(
54:     dir ? resolve(dir, '../../project-migrations') : undefined,
55:     embeddedProjectMigrations(),
56:     'project migrations',
57:   );
58: }

Read file backend/packages/core/src/db/migrate.ts, lines 1-82
1: import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, copyFileSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
2: import { resolve } from 'node:path';
3: import type { Database } from './connection.js';
4: 
5: /**
6:  * One SQL migration as an in-memory entry — the form the single-file SEA
7:  * executable uses (no .sql files exist next to the binary; see D-011).
8:  * `name` matches the on-disk file name so the `__migrations` ledger is
9:  * identical whichever source applied it.
10:  */
11: export interface MigrationFile {
12:   name: string;
13:   sql: string;
14: }
15: 
16: /**
17:  * Applies pending SQL migrations to a database, each inside its own transaction with its
18:  * own pre-migration backup (`<db>.<migration file>.bak`).
19:  *
20:  * Used twice: for the registry DB (`migrations/`) and lazily for every project DB
21:  * (`project-migrations/`).
22:  *
23:  * `source` is either a directory on disk (dev, Docker — reads `.sql` files) or an
24:  * embedded array (SEA executable). Disk content and embedded content are generated
25:  * from the same files, so both forms produce the same ledger.
26:  */
27: export function runMigrations(db: Database, source: string | MigrationFile[]): void {
28:   db.raw.exec(`
29:     CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS __migrations (
30:       id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
31:       name TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
32:       applied_at TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
33:     )
34:   `);
35: 
36:   const applied = new Set(
37:     db.raw.prepare('SELECT name FROM __migrations').all().map((r: any) => r.name),
38:   );
39: 
40:   let files: string[];
41:   let sqlOf: (name: string) => string;
42:   if (typeof source === 'string') {
43:     files = readdirSync(source)
44:       .filter((f) => f.endsWith('.sql'))
45:       .sort();
46:     sqlOf = (name) => readFileSync(resolve(source, name), 'utf-8');
47:   } else {
48:     const byName = new Map(source.map((f) => [f.name, f.sql]));
49:     files = [...byName.keys()].sort();
50:     sqlOf = (name) => {
51:       const sql = byName.get(name);
52:       if (sql === undefined) throw new Error(`Embedded migration ${name} has no SQL content.`);
53:       return sql;
54:     };
55:   }
56: 
57:   for (const file of files) {
58:     if (applied.has(file)) continue;
59: 
60:     const backupPath = `${db.path}.${file}.bak`;
61:     if (!existsSync(backupPath)) {
62:       copyFileSync(db.path, backupPath);
63:     }
64: 
65:     const statements = sqlOf(file);
66: 
67:     db.raw.exec('BEGIN');
68:     try {
69:       db.raw.exec(statements);
70:       db.raw.prepare('INSERT INTO __migrations (name) VALUES (?)').run(file);
71:       db.raw.exec('COMMIT');
72:     } catch (err) {
73:       db.raw.exec('ROLLBACK');
74:       throw err;
75:     }
76:   }
77: }
78: 
79: /** Ensure a migrations directory exists (used for per-project databases). */
80: export function ensureMigrationsDir(dir: string): void {
81:   if (!existsSync(dir)) mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
82: }

1: /**
2:  * SQLite stores JSON columns as TEXT; the shared response schemas promise parsed values
3:  * (`acceptance_criteria: z.array(string)`, `payload: z.record(...)` etc.). Every read path
4:  * maps rows through these helpers so the wire format matches the contract — the dashboard
5:  * and MCP clients must never see JSON text. Parsing is idempotent: an already-parsed
6:  * value passes through unchanged.
7:  */
8: export function parseJsonColumn<T>(value: unknown, fallback: T): T {
9:   if (typeof value === 'string') {
10:     try {
11:       return JSON.parse(value) as T;
12:     } catch {
13:       return fallback;
14:     }
15:   }
16:   return (value ?? fallback) as T;
17: }
18: 
19: export function toTaskRow<T extends Record<string, any>>(row: T) {
20:   return { ...row, acceptance_criteria: parseJsonColumn<string[]>(row.acceptance_criteria, []) };
21: }
22: 
23: export function toEventRow<T extends Record<string, any>>(row: T) {
24:   return { ...row, payload: parseJsonColumn<Record<string, unknown>>(row.payload, {}) };
25: }
26: 
27: export function toCheckpointRow<T extends Record<string, any>>(row: T) {

19: declare module 'fastify' {
20:   interface FastifyInstance {
21:     /** Registry DB (project index + project-level idempotency). */
22:     registryDb: Database;
23:     /** Opens per-project databases on demand. */
24:     projectDbs: ProjectDbResolver;
25:     /** Registry-scoped services; task-scoped routes use `buildServices(projectDb)`. */
26:     services: CoreServices;
27:     config: Config;
28:   }
29: }
30: 
31: export interface BuildServerOptions {
32:   /**
33:    * When provided, the dashboard is served from these in-memory assets on the
34:    * same origin as the API (`--with-ui` / SEA executable). Omitted → API-only,
35:    * exactly the previous behaviour.
36:    */
37:   ui?: UiAssets | null;
38: }
39: 
40: export function buildServer(db: Database, config: Config, options: BuildServerOptions = {}) {
41:   // Startup gate: PROJECTS_DIRECTORY configured but the container mount is
42:   // missing/unreadable → actionable failure before any request is served.
43:   const mountError = validateProjectsMount();
44:   if (mountError) throw new Error(mountError);
45: 
46:   const server = Fastify({
47:     logger: { level: config.logLevel },
48:     bodyLimit: 1_048_576,
49:   });
50: 
51:   const projectDbs = new ProjectDbResolver(db, projectMigrationsSource());
52: 
53:   server.decorate('registryDb', db);
54:   server.decorate('projectDbs', projectDbs);
55:   server.decorate('services', buildServices(db, config.pathContext));
56:   server.decorate('config', config);
57: 
58:   server.addHook('onClose', async () => {
59:     projectDbs.closeAll();
60:   });
61: 
62:   server.setErrorHandler((error: any, request, reply) => {
63:     if (error?.name === 'WorkJournalError') {
64:       return reply
65:         .status(error.statusCode || 400)
66:         .send({ error: error.code, message: error.message });
67:     }
68:     request.log.error(error);
69:     return reply.status(500).send({ error: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'An internal error occurred' });
70:   });
71: 
72:   server.register(registerProjectRoutes, { prefix: '/api/projects' });
73:   server.register(registerTaskRoutes, { prefix: '/api/tasks' });
74:   server.register(registerEventRoutes, { prefix: '/api/events' });
75:   server.register(registerCheckpointRoutes, { prefix: '/api/checkpoints' });
76:   server.register(registerDecisionRoutes, { prefix: '/api/decisions' });
77:   server.register(registerResumeRoutes, { prefix: '/api/resume' });
78:   server.register(registerExportRoutes, { prefix: '/api/export' });
79:   server.register(registerDashboardRoutes, { prefix: '/api/dashboard' });
80: 
81:   // Liveness probe for container health checks: no DB access, no auth (root
82:   // AGENTS.md: no authentication; the route reveals nothing).
83:   server.get('/health', async () => ({ status: 'ok' }));
84: 
85:   if (options.ui) registerUiRoutes(server, options.ui);
86: 
87:   return server;
88: }

1: /**
2:  * Build-time codegen: embeds the committed SQL migrations into
3:  * `src/db/embedded-migrations.generated.ts` so the SEA executable can run
4:  * migrations with no files on disk (decision D-011).
5:  *
6:  * Run via `pnpm --filter @workjournal/core embed` (chained into `build`).
7:  * Unlike the UI embed, a missing migrations directory is a hard error — those
8:  * files are committed source, absence means a broken checkout.
9:  *
10:  * The output file is COMMITTED for the same reason as the UI embed: a fresh
11:  * clone must typecheck and build before any codegen has run.
12:  */
13: import { readdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
14: import { dirname, join, relative, resolve } from 'node:path';
15: import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
16: 
17: const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)); // packages/core/scripts/
18: const outFile = resolve(here, '../src/db/embedded-migrations.generated.ts');
19: 
20: interface DirSpec {
21:   /** Exported function name in the generated module. */
22:   exportName: string;
23:   dir: string;
24: }
25: 
26: const specs: DirSpec[] = [
27:   { exportName: 'embeddedRegistryMigrations', dir: resolve(here, '../migrations') },
28:   { exportName: 'embeddedProjectMigrations', dir: resolve(here, '../project-migrations') },
29: ];
30: 
31: function readMigrations(dir: string): { name: string; sql: string }[] {
32:   let names: string[];
33:   try {
34:     names = readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.sql')).sort();
35:   } catch (err) {
36:     throw new Error(
37:       `embed-migrations: cannot read ${dir} — the committed SQL migrations are required ` +
38:         `(${err instanceof Error ? err.message : err}).`,
39:     );
40:   }
41:   if (names.length === 0) {
42:     throw new Error(`embed-migrations: no .sql files in ${dir} — refusing to embed an empty set.`);
43:   }
44:   return names.map((name) => ({ name, sql: readFileSync(join(dir, name), 'utf-8') }));
45: }
46: 
47: const functions = specs
48:   .map(({ exportName, dir }) => {
49:     const entries = readMigrations(dir);
50:     const rows = entries
51:       .map((e) => `    { name: ${JSON.stringify(e.name)}, sql: ${JSON.stringify(e.sql)} }`)
52:       .join(',\n');
53:     return (
54:       `export function ${exportName}(): MigrationFile[] {\n` +
55:       `  return [\n${rows},\n  ];\n}`
56:     );
57:   })
58:   .join('\n\n');
59: 
60: const output = `// GENERATED by packages/core/scripts/embed-migrations.ts — do not edit by hand.
61: // Regenerate with \`pnpm --filter @workjournal/core embed\`. Committed
62: // intentionally (decision D-011): a fresh clone must typecheck and build
63: // without running codegen first.
64: import type { MigrationFile } from './migrate.js';
65: 
66: ${functions}
67: `;
68: 
69: writeFileSync(outFile, output);
70: const summary = specs
71:   .map(({ exportName, dir }) => {
72:     const count = readMigrations(dir).length;
73:     return `${exportName}=${count} (${relative(resolve(here, '../../../..'), dir)})`;
74:   })
75:   .join(', ');
76: process.stdout.write(`embed-migrations: wrote ${relative(resolve(here, '../../../..'), outFile)} — ${summary}\n`);

### WS-2.3 — EventService append()

Design:

```ts
export interface AppendEventInput {
  projectId: string;
  taskId: string | null;
  actor: string;
  source: string;         // 'core' | 'mcp_tool' | 'agent:<id>'
  eventType: string;
  payload: unknown;
  eventId?: string | null;  // client-generated dedup key (ingestion); null for core events
  occurredAt?: string;      // UTC; default now
}

append(input): { id, event_id, sequence, created_at, duplicate? } 
```

Behavior:
1. Validate event type: isEventType → validate payload against schema (existing behavior). Unknown type → for core callers, keep free-form object validation (existing behavior allowed arbitrary types like 'work_started' — see handoff-flow.test.ts line 41 uses eventType 'work_started'!). Important: existing test uses free-form 'work_started'. So core recordEvent keeps permissive free-form for unknown types; ingestion rejects non-allowlisted types. Keep that split: append() validates known types strictly, unknown types require plain object (existing behavior). Ingestion route checks isIngestibleType first →400 otherwise.
2. eventId dedup: if eventId provided, SELECT existing by event_id → return stored with duplicate flag.
3. Sequence: if taskId: MAX(sequence) WHERE task_id = ?; else MAX(sequence) WHERE task_id IS NULL AND project_id = ? (project-level events share per-project sequence namespace).

Wait — subtlety: per-project sequence for NULL task events. But then a task's sequence and project-level sequence can collide — that's fine, they're separate scopes; ordering within a scope is monotonic. The index (project_id, task_id, sequence) supports the project-level query (task_id NULL). OK.

4. Insert with category derived via categoryForEventType (unknown types → 'task'? Hmm — free-form types like 'work_started' get category... existing rows default 'task'. For unknown free-form types, category 'task' seems wrong but it's the default per migration. Actually free-form event types are legacy path; map unknown → 'task' (matches migration default). Fine.)

source: recordEvent callers (task-service etc.) pass source 'core' (default). 

Public API preservation: recordEvent(data: {projectId, taskId, actor, eventType, payload}) → delegates to append with source 'core', eventId null. 8 existing callers unchanged. 

Convenience wrappers per M3 §5: recordAgentEvent, recordProgressEvent, recordVerificationEvent, recordRecoveryEvent — thin wrappers calling append with proper source/actor. They funnel through append. (Spec names them; implement as thin wrappers.)

Transaction handling: existing recordEvent is called INSIDE caller transactions ("Never called inside its own transaction: the caller owns the transaction"). Same for append: no own transaction; callers wrap. For ingestion, the ingest service/route wraps each event (or the batch) in a transaction.

Idempotency uniqueness enforcement: UNIQUE partial index on event_id; on conflict (race), catch SQLITE_CONSTRAINT → re-select and return duplicate. Good for concurrent retries.

135:         if (updates.priority !== undefined) { sets.push('priority = ?'); params.push(updates.priority); changed.push('priority'); }
136:         if (updates.acceptanceCriteria !== undefined) {
137:           sets.push('acceptance_criteria = ?');
138:           params.push(JSON.stringify(updates.acceptanceCriteria));
139:           changed.push('acceptance_criteria');
140:         }
141: 
142:         params.push(taskId, expectedVersion);
143:         this.db.raw
144:           .prepare(`UPDATE tasks SET ${sets.join(', ')} WHERE id = ? AND version = ?`)
145:           .run(...params);
146: 
147:         this.events.recordEvent({
148:           projectId: task.project_id,
149:           taskId,
150:           actor: agentId,
151:           eventType: 'task_updated',
152:           payload: { fields: changed },
153:         });
154: 
155:         return this.getTask(taskId);
156:       },
157:     );
158:   }
159: 
160:   /**
161:    * Claiming runs through the transition function, so it is permitted exactly where
162:    * `in_progress` is a legal next status: from `open` (owner must be free) and from
163:    * `interrupted` (the previous attempt ended, so the claim is reassigned to the
164:    * recovering agent â€” scope Â§18 continuity, Agent A â†’ Agent B).
165:    */
166:   async claimTask(taskId: string, agentId: string, expectedVersion: number, idempotency?: MutationIdempotency) {
167:     return this.idem.run(
168:       'idempotency_records',
169:       idempotency && { ...idempotency, operation: 'task.claim', actor: agentId },
170:       () => {
171:         const task = this.getTask(taskId);
172:         if (!canTransition(task.status, TaskStatus.InProgress)) {
173:           throw new InvalidTransitionError(
174:             `Cannot claim a task in status "${task.status}". Only an open or interrupted task can be claimed.`,
175:           );
176:         }
177:         if (task.version !== expectedVersion) {
178:           throw new StaleVersionError(`Expected version ${expectedVersion}, found ${task.version}. Re-read the task and retry.`);
179:         }
180: 
181:         const recovery = task.status === TaskStatus.Interrupted;
182:         if (task.owner && !recovery) {
183:           throw new TaskClaimedError(`Task is already claimed by ${task.owner}.`);
184:         }
185: 
186:         const now = new Date().toISOString();
187:         this.db.raw
188:           .prepare(
189:             `UPDATE tasks SET owner = ?, status = ?, version = version + 1, updated_at = ?
190:              WHERE id = ? AND version = ?`,
191:           )
192:           .run(agentId, TaskStatus.InProgress, now, taskId, expectedVersion);
193: 
194:         this.events.recordEvent({
195:           projectId: task.project_id,
196:           taskId,
197:           actor: agentId,
198:           eventType: 'task_claimed',
199:           payload: { owner: agentId },
200:         });
201: 
202:         return this.getTask(taskId);
203:       },
204:     );
205:   }
206: 
207:   async releaseTask(taskId: string, agentId: string, expectedVersion: number, idempotency?: MutationIdempotency) {
208:     return this.idem.run(
209:       'idempotency_records',
210:       idempotency && { ...idempotency, operation: 'task.release', actor: agentId },
211:       () => {
212:         const task = this.getTask(taskId);
213:         if (task.owner !== agentId) {
214:           throw new OwnerMismatchError(
215:             task.owner ? `Task is owned by ${task.owner}.` : 'Task has no owner to release.',
216:           );
217:         }
218:         if (task.version !== expectedVersion) {
219:           throw new StaleVersionError(`Expected version ${expectedVersion}, found ${task.version}. Re-read the task and retry.`);
220:         }
221:         if (!canTransition(task.status, TaskStatus.Open)) {
222:           throw new InvalidTransitionError(`Cannot release a task in status "${task.status}".`);
223:         }
224: 
225:         const now = new Date().toISOString();
226:         this.db.raw
227:           .prepare(
228:             `UPDATE tasks SET owner = NULL, status = ?, version = version + 1, updated_at = ?
229:              WHERE id = ? AND version = ?`,
230:           )
231:           .run(TaskStatus.Open, now, taskId, expectedVersion);
232: 
233:         this.events.recordEvent({
234:           projectId: task.project_id,
235:           taskId,
236:           actor: agentId,
237:           eventType: 'task_released',
238:           payload: { previous_owner: agentId },
239:         });
240: 
241:         return this.getTask(taskId);
242:       },
243:     );
244:   }
245: 
246:   async transitionTask(
247:     taskId: string,
248:     agentId: string,
249:     expectedVersion: number,
250:     targetStatus: string,
251:     reason?: string,
252:     resolutionNote?: string,
253:     idempotency?: MutationIdempotency,
254:   ) {
255:     return this.idem.run(
256:       'idempotency_records',
257:       idempotency && { ...idempotency, operation: 'task.transition', actor: agentId },
258:       () => {
259:         const task = this.getTask(taskId);
260: 
261:         if (task.owner !== agentId) {
262:           throw new OwnerMismatchError(
263:             task.owner ? `Task is owned by ${task.owner}.` : 'Task is unclaimed; claim it before transitioning it.',
264:           );
265:         }
266:         if (task.version !== expectedVersion) {
267:           throw new StaleVersionError(`Expected version ${expectedVersion}, found ${task.version}. Re-read the task and retry.`);
268:         }
269: 
270:         // Scope Â§5: cancellation, blocking and interruption require a reason; unblocking
271:         // records a resolution note.
272:         if ([TaskStatus.Cancelled, TaskStatus.Blocked, TaskStatus.Interrupted].includes(targetStatus as any)
273:           && !reason?.trim()) {
274:           throw new ValidationError(`Transition to "${targetStatus}" requires a reason.`);
275:         }
276:         if (targetStatus === TaskStatus.Open && !resolutionNote?.trim()) {
277:           throw new ValidationError('Unblocking requires a resolution note.');
278:         }
279: 
280:         if (!canTransition(task.status, targetStatus as any)) {
281:           throw new InvalidTransitionError(
282:             `Cannot transition from "${task.status}" to "${targetStatus}".`,
283:           );
284:         }
285: 
286:         const now = new Date().toISOString();
287:         this.db.raw
288:           .prepare('UPDATE tasks SET status = ?, version = version + 1, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ? AND version = ?')
289:           .run(targetStatus, now, taskId, expectedVersion);
290: 
291:         this.events.recordEvent({
292:           projectId: task.project_id,
293:           taskId,
294:           actor: agentId,
295:           eventType: 'task_transitioned',
296:           payload: {
297:             from: task.status,
298:             to: targetStatus,
299:             ...(reason ? { reason } : {}),
300:             ...(resolutionNote ? { resolution_note: resolutionNote } : {}),
301:           },
302:         });
303: 
304:         return this.getTask(taskId);
305:       },
306:     );
307:   }
308: }

1: export async function callCore<T = any>(
2:   baseUrl: string,
3:   method: string,
4:   path: string,
5:   body?: unknown,
6: ): Promise<T> {
7:   const url = `${baseUrl}${path}`;
8: 
9:   const options: RequestInit = {
10:     method,
11:     headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
12:   };
13: 
14:   if (body !== undefined && method !== 'GET') {
15:     options.body = JSON.stringify(body);
16:   }
17: 
18:   let response: Response;
19:   try {
20:     response = await fetch(url, options);
21:   } catch (err: any) {
22:     // Root AGENTS.md: if the service is down, tell the agent how to start it; no auto-start.
23:     throw new Error(
24:       `WorkJournal core service is not reachable at ${baseUrl} (${err?.message || err}). ` +
25:         `Start it with "pnpm start" (service) or "pnpm dev" (watch mode) in backend/, then retry.`,
26:     );
27:   }
28: 
29:   const text = await response.text();
30:   let data: any = null;
31:   if (text) {
32:     try {
33:       data = JSON.parse(text);
34:     } catch {
35:       data = null; // non-JSON body (e.g. text/markdown exports)
36:     }
37:   }
38: 
39:   if (!response.ok) {
40:     const code = typeof data?.error === 'string' ? data.error : undefined;
41:     const message = data?.message || (typeof data?.error === 'object' && data?.error?.message) ||
42:       `Core service error: ${response.status}`;
43:     throw new Error(code ? `[${code}] ${message}` : String(message));
44:   }
45: 
46:   // Markdown exports come back as text; everything else is JSON.
47:   return (data === null ? (text as unknown as T) : (data as T));
48: }

27:  * Normalise a stored root_path for deterministic comparison across path
28:  * forms (git-style `/c/...` vs drive-style `C:/...`, trailing slashes, etc.).
29:  * Both sides are run through normalizeRootPath → normalizeProjectPath.
30:  */
31: export function semanticRootPath(path: string): string {
32:   return normalizeProjectPath(normalizeRootPath(path));
33: }
34: 
35: /**
36:  * Read the `projects` table from an existing project DB file without creating
37:  * files, running migrations or caching the connection (root AGENTS.md: the
38:  * project DB is authoritative). Returns [] when the file is missing, the
39:  * `projects` table has no rows yet, or any read failure occurs — registration
40:  * should fall back to a fresh uuid in those cases.
41:  *
42:  * The caller passes the preferred root path (the newly-typed registration path) which is
43:  * used to favour a row whose stored root_path (normalised) matches the incoming
44:  * path; otherwise the first row by creation order is chosen.
45:  */
46: export function readProjectRows(dbPath: string): Array<Record<string, any>> {
47:   if (!existsSync(dbPath)) return [];
48:   let db: Database;
49:   try {
50:     db = createDatabase(dbPath);
51:   } catch {
52:     return [];
53:   }
54:   try {
55:     return db.raw
56:       .prepare('SELECT * FROM projects ORDER BY created_at ASC, id ASC')
57:       .all() as Array<Record<string, any>>;
58:   } catch {
59:     return []; // DB file exists but migrations never ran → no projects table
60:   } finally {
61:     try {
62:       db.raw.close();
63:     } catch {
64:       // already closed
65:     }
66:   }
67: }
68: 
69: /**
70:  * Given the rows from readProjectRows() and a preferred root path (the
71:  * newly-typed registration path), return the single authoritative row or null.
72:  * A row whose normalised root_path matches the preferred path wins; otherwise
73:  * the first (oldest) row by creation order is chosen.
74:  */
75: export function pickProjectRow(
76:   rows: Array<Record<string, any>>,
77:   preferredRootPath: string
78: ): Record<string, any> | null {
79:   if (!rows || rows.length === 0) return null;
80:   const preferred = semanticRootPath(preferredRootPath);
81:   const match = rows.find((r) => semanticRootPath(r.root_path) === preferred);
82:   if (match) return match;
83:   // No exact spelling match — oldest wins.
84:   return rows[0];
85: }
86: 
87: /**
88:  * Given a preferred root path (newly-typed registration folder), read the
89:  * authoritative project row from an existing project DB or return null if
90:  * there is none / the DB cannot be read.  Never creates files or runs migrations
91:  * (root AGENTS.md: the project DB is authoritative and lazily initialized).
92:  */
93: export function readExistingProjectRow(
94:   dbPath: string,
95:   preferredRootPath: string
96: ): Record<string, any> | null {
97:   const rows = readProjectRows(dbPath);
98:   return pickProjectRow(rows, preferredRootPath);
99: }
100: 
101: export interface ProjectScope {
102:   projectId: string;
103:   db: Database;
104: }
105: 
106: /**
107:  * Opens per-project databases on demand (registry DB stays the index only).
108:  *
109:  * - `forProject` creates and migrates `<root>/.workjournal/workjournal.db`, then makes
110:  *   the project row authoritative in that database.
111:  * - `forTask` / `forCheckpoint` / `forDecision` locate the project DB that holds a record
112:  *   by scanning registered projects, skipping unavailable (missing) folders.
113:  */
114: export class ProjectDbResolver {
115:   private cache = new Map<string, Database>();
116: 
117:   constructor(
118:     private registry: Database,
119:     private projectMigrations: string | MigrationFile[],
120:   ) {}
121: 
122:   /** Registry row for a project, or an actionable NOT_FOUND. */
123:   projectRow(projectId: string): Record<string, any> {
124:     const row = this.registry.raw
125:       .prepare('SELECT * FROM registry_projects WHERE id = ?')
126:       .get(projectId) as any;
127:     if (!row) throw new NotFoundError(`Project ${projectId} not found`);
128:     return row;
129:   }
130: 
131:   /** True when the project folder is reachable right now. */
132:   isAvailable(rootPath: string): boolean {
133:     return existsSync(normalizeRootPath(rootPath));
134:   }
135: 
136:   forProject(projectId: string): Database {
137:     const row = this.projectRow(projectId);
138:     const dbPath = projectDbPath(row.root_path);
139: 
140:     if (!this.isAvailable(row.root_path)) {
141:       throw new NotFoundError(
142:         `Project "${row.name}" is unavailable: folder ${normalizeRootPath(row.root_path)} does not exist. Re-register the project from its current root path.`,
143:       );
144:     }
145: 
146:     const db = this.open(projectId, dbPath);
147:     this.syncProjectRow(db, row);
148:     return db;
149:   }
150: 
151:   /** Locate the project DB that contains a task. Skips unavailable projects. */
152:   forTask(taskId: string): ProjectScope {
153:     const scope = this.scan('tasks', taskId);
154:     if (!scope) throw new NotFoundError(`Task ${taskId} not found`);
155:     return scope;
156:   }
157: 
158:   forCheckpoint(checkpointId: string): ProjectScope {
159:     const scope = this.scan('checkpoints', checkpointId);
160:     if (!scope) throw new NotFoundError(`Checkpoint ${checkpointId} not found`);
161:     return scope;
162:   }
163: 
164:   forDecision(decisionId: string): ProjectScope {
165:     const scope = this.scan('decisions', decisionId);
166:     if (!scope) throw new NotFoundError(`Decision ${decisionId} not found`);
167:     return scope;
168:   }
169: 
170:   closeAll(): void {
171:     for (const db of this.cache.values()) db.raw.close();
172:     this.cache.clear();
173:   }
174: 
175:   /** Re-sync the project row into an already-open project DB (after a registry edit). */
176:   refreshIfOpen(projectId: string): boolean {
177:     const db = this.cache.get(projectId);
178:     if (!db) return false;
179:     this.syncProjectRow(db, this.projectRow(projectId));
180:     return true;
181:   }
182: 
183:   /** Every registered project, oldest first — the dashboard scope dropdown. */
184:   allProjects(): Array<Record<string, any>> {
185:     return this.registry.raw
186:       .prepare('SELECT * FROM registry_projects ORDER BY created_at ASC')
187:       .all() as any[];
188:   }
189: 
190:   /** Registered projects whose folder currently exists on disk. */
191:   availableProjects(): Array<Record<string, any>> {
192:     const rows = this.registry.raw
193:       .prepare('SELECT * FROM registry_projects ORDER BY created_at ASC')
194:       .all() as any[];
195:     return rows.filter((row) => this.isAvailable(row.root_path));
196:   }
197: 
198:   private scan(table: 'tasks' | 'checkpoints' | 'decisions', id: string): ProjectScope | null {
199:     const rows = this.registry.raw
200:       .prepare('SELECT * FROM registry_projects ORDER BY created_at ASC')
201:       .all() as any[];
202: 
203:     for (const row of rows) {
204:       const dbPath = projectDbPath(row.root_path);
205:       // No project DB yet ⇒ no records in it; never create files while scanning.
206:       if (!existsSync(dbPath)) continue;
207: 
208:       const db = this.open(row.id, dbPath);
209:       // Table names are fixed literals here — only id values are bound parameters.
210:       const statement =
211:         table === 'tasks'
212:           ? db.raw.prepare('SELECT id FROM tasks WHERE id = ?')
213:           : table === 'checkpoints'
214:             ? db.raw.prepare('SELECT id FROM checkpoints WHERE id = ?')
215:             : db.raw.prepare('SELECT id FROM decisions WHERE id = ?');
216:       const found = statement.get(id) as any;
217:       if (found) {
218:         this.syncProjectRow(db, row);
219:         return { projectId: row.id, db };
220:       }
221:     }
222:     return null;
223:   }
224: 
225:   private open(projectId: string, dbPath: string): Database {
226:     const cached = this.cache.get(projectId);
227:     if (cached) return cached;
228: 
229:     const db = createDatabase(dbPath);
230:     runMigrations(db, this.projectMigrations);
231:     this.cache.set(projectId, db);
232:     return db;
233:   }
234: 
235:   /** The project DB copy of the project row is authoritative once open. */
236:   private syncProjectRow(db: Database, row: Record<string, any>): void {
237:     db.raw
238:       .prepare(
239:         `INSERT INTO projects (id, name, description, root_path, created_at, updated_at)
240:          VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
241:          ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET
242:            name = excluded.name,
243:            description = excluded.description,
244:            root_path = excluded.root_path,
245:            updated_at = excluded.updated_at`,
246:       )
247:       .run(
248:         row.id,
249:         row.name,
250:         row.description ?? null,
251:         row.root_path,
252:         row.created_at,
253:         row.updated_at,
254:       );
255:   }
256: }

import type { TaskService } from './task-service.js';
import type { ProjectService } from './project-service.js';
import type { CheckpointService } from './checkpoint-service.js';
import type { DecisionService } from './decision-service.js';

import type { MarkdownExporter } from '../export/markdown-export.js';

/** Service bundle bound to one database (the registry, or one project DB). */

  tasks: TaskService;
  projects: ProjectService;
  checkpoints: CheckpointService;
  decisions: DecisionService;
  events: EventService;
  resume: ResumeBuilder;
  jsonExport: JsonExporter;
  markdownExport: MarkdownExporter;
}

### 1. `backend/packages/core/project-migrations/002_unified_events.sql`

```sql
-- 002_unified_events.sql — canonical event model (plan §3 / spec M3, WS-2)
--
-- Rebuilds `events` so that:
--   * task_id becomes NULLABLE (project-level agent events have no task, plan §3.1);
--   * event_id is the client-generated dedup key for idempotent ingestion (D-023);
--   * occurred_at separates event occurrence from recording time (M3 §2);
--   * category and source classify every event for the unified timeline (D-017).
-- Existing rows keep category 'task' and source 'core'; occurred_at = created_at.
-- SQLite cannot alter constraints in place, hence the table rebuild.

CREATE TABLE events_rebuilt (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  project_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  task_id TEXT REFERENCES tasks(id),
  sequence INTEGER NOT NULL,
  actor TEXT NOT NULL,
  event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
  category TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'task',
  source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'core',
  payload TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  event_id TEXT,
  occurred_at TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL
);

INSERT INTO events_rebuilt (id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, category, source, payload, event_id, occurred_at, created_at)
  SELECT id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, 'task', 'core', payload, NULL, created_at, created_at
  FROM events;

DROP TABLE events;
ALTER TABLE events_rebuilt RENAME TO events;

CREATE INDEX idx_events_task ON events(task_id, sequence);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_project ON events(project_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_project_task_seq ON events(project_id, task_id, sequence);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_task_category ON events(task_id, category, sequence);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_events_event_id ON events(event_id) WHERE event_id IS NOT NULL;
```

Wait — one caveat: runMigrations wraps exec in BEGIN/COMMIT. DROP TABLE inside a transaction with foreign_keys=ON — SQLite handles DROP TABLE specially: it performs an implicit "DELETE FROM" and if FK constraints from the dropped table exist... events has FK → tasks (child side). Dropping the child table is fine. FK enforcement of the INSERT into events_rebuilt: task_id values reference tasks — valid. OK.

Another caveat: PRAGMA foreign_keys is ON (connection.ts). With FK on, `DROP TABLE events` while other tables reference `events`? None do in 001. checkpoint_sources in WS-4 comes later and will reference the NEW events table (already renamed). OK.

Also: within a transaction, ALTER TABLE RENAME is allowed. RENAME updates references in other tables/triggers/views — none reference events. OK.

### 2. `002b_session_task_binding.sql`

```sql
-- 002b_session_task_binding.sql — session↔task correlation map (plan §2/D-023, WS-2.5)
--
-- The MCP adapter forwards OpenCode's ai.opencode/sessionID tool-call _meta; a
-- successful claim-like tool call records a binding so later plugin ingestion-- events for the same session are backfilled with task_id. Events without an
-- active binding stay project-level (task_id NULL, plan §3.1).

CREATE TABLE session_task_bindings (
  session_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  project_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  task_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES tasks(id),
  agent_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
  released_at TEXT
);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_session_bindings_active ON session_task_bindings(session_id) WHERE released_at IS NULL;
CREATE INDEX idx_session_bindings_task ON session_task_bindings(task_id);
```

### 3. shared/src/types/events.ts (rewrite)

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';

/** Event types recorded in the append-only per-task history (scope §9). */
export const EVENT_TYPES = [
  // task — authoritative lifecycle (core only)
  'task_created', 'task_updated', 'task_claimed', 'task_released', 'task_transitioned',
  // decision
  'decision_recorded', 'decision_superseded',
  // recovery
  'checkpoint_recorded', 'recovery_started', 'recovery_completed',
  // agent (plan §3.2)
  'agent_session_started', 'agent_session_ended', 'agent_interrupted', 'agent_timed_out', 'agent_idle',
  // progress
  'progress_milestone', 'progress_fulfilment',
  // verification
  'verification_passed', 'verification_failed', 'verification_skipped',
] as const;

export type EventType = (typeof EVENT_TYPES)[number];

export const EVENT_CATEGORIES = ['task','agent','progress','decision','verification','recovery'] as const;
export type EventCategory = (typeof EVENT_CATEGORIES)[number];

/** Single source of truth: category derived from event type (never client-supplied). */
export const EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE = {
  task_created:'task', task_updated:'task', task_claimed:'task', task_released:'task', task_transitioned:'task',
  decision_recorded:'decision', decision_superseded:'decision',
  checkpoint_recorded:'recovery', recovery_started:'recovery', recovery_completed:'recovery',
  agent_session_started:'agent', agent_session_ended:'agent', agent_interrupted:'agent', agent_timed_out:'agent', agent_idle:'agent',
  progress_milestone:'progress', progress_fulfilment:'progress',
  verification_passed:'verification', verification_failed:'verification', verification_skipped:'verification',
} as const satisfies Record<EventType, EventCategory>;

export function categoryForEventType(eventType: string): EventCategory {
  return (EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE as Record<string, EventCategory>)[eventType] ?? 'task';
}
```

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';

/** Source syntax per D-017: 'mcp_tool' or 'agent:<agent-id>'; 'core' is never accepted from clients. */
const ingestSource = z.string().min(1).max(128).regex(/^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_.:-]*$/).refine(s => s !== 'core', 'source "core" is reserved for Core-generated events');

export const ingestEnvelopeSchema = z.object({
  event_id: z.string().min(1).max(200),
  type: z.string().min(1).max(100),
  source: ingestSource,
  actor: z.string().min(1).max(200),
  task_id: z.string().min(1).max(200).optional(),
  occurred_at: z.string().datetime().optional(),  // UTC required (invariant #7)
  payload: z.record(z.unknown()),
});

export const ingestBatchSchema = z.object({
  events: z.array(ingestEnvelopeSchema).min(1).max(50),
});

export const ingestEventResultSchema = z.object({
  event_id: z.string(),
  status: z.enum(['accepted','duplicate','rejected']),
  id: z.string().optional(),
  sequence: z.number().int().optional(),
  task_id: z.string().nullable().optional(),
  reason: z.string().optional(),
});

export const ingestBatchResultSchema = z.object({
  results: z.array(ingestEventResultSchema),
  accepted: z.number().int(),
  duplicates: z.number().int(),
  rejected: z.number().int(),
});
```

Where do agent-tool input schemas go? shared/src/schemas/agent-events.ts:
```ts
import { z } from 'zod';

const agentId = z.string().min(1).max(200);
const sessionId = z.string().min(1).max(200);
const taskId = z.string().min(1).max(200);
const idem = z.string().min(1).max(128).regex(/^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$/);

export const startAgentSessionSchema = z.object({ agent_id, session_id, idempotency_key: idem, task_id: taskId.optional() });
export const endAgentSessionSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({ reason: z.enum(['completed','interrupted','failed','timeout']) });
export const reportInterruptionSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({ reason: z.string().min(1).max(2000).optional() });
export const reportTimeoutSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({ timeout_ms: z.number().int().nonnegative().optional() });
export const reportProgressMilestoneSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({ summary: z.string().min(1).max(2000) });
export const reportFulfilmentSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({
  summary: ..., findings: optional, blockers: optional, next_steps: optional, files_changed: optional array,
});
export const reportVerificationSchema = startAgentSessionSchema.extend({
  outcome: z.enum(['passed','failed','skipped']), check_type: z.string().min(1).max(200), detail: optional,
});
```

The adapter builds envelopes from these: type = tool-specific, event_id = idempotency_key, source = `agent:${agent_id}`, actor = agent_id, payload = fields minus common.

### 5. core event-service.ts rewrite

```ts
import { v4 as uuid } from 'uuid';
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { Database } from '../db/connection.js';
import { isEventType, parseEventPayload, categoryForEventType } from '@workjournal/shared';
import { toEventRow } from './serialize.js';

export interface RecordEventInput {  // legacy shape kept for core callers
  projectId: string; taskId: string; actor: string; eventType: string; payload: unknown;
}

export interface AppendEventInput {
  projectId: string;
  taskId: string | null;
  actor: string;
  source: string;
  eventType: string;
  payload: unknown;
  eventId?: string | null;
  occurredAt?: string | null;
}

export interface AppendedEvent {
  id: string; event_id: string | null; sequence: number; created_at: string; occurred_at: string; duplicate: boolean;
}

export interface ListEventsFilter {
  taskId?: string; projectId?: string; category?: string; source?: string; limit?: number; offset?: number;
}

const objectPayload = z.record(z.unknown());

function validatePayload(...) — same as before

export class EventService {
  constructor(private db: Database) {}

  /** THE funnel (M3 §5): sequence, validation, dedup and scope in one place. */
  append(input: AppendEventInput): AppendedEvent {
    const payload = validatePayload(input.eventType, input.payload);
    const category = categoryForEventType(input.eventType);

    if (input.eventId) {
      const existing = this.findByEventId(input.eventId);
      if (existing) return { ...appendedFromRow(existing), duplicate: true };
    }

    const id = uuid();
    const now = new Date().toISOString();
    const occurredAt = input.occurredAt ?? now;
    const sequence = this.nextSequence(input.projectId, input.taskId);

    try {
      this.db.raw.prepare(
        `INSERT INTO events (id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, category, source, payload, event_id, occurred_at, created_at)
         VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,
      ).run(id, input.projectId, input.taskId ?? null, sequence, input.actor, input.eventType, category, input.source, JSON.stringify(payload), input.eventId ?? null, occurredAt, now);
    } catch (err: any) {
      // Concurrent retry with the same event_id: the unique index won the race.
      if (input.eventId && String(err?.code ?? '').startsWith('SQLITE_CONSTRAINT')) {
        const existing = this.findByEventId(input.eventId);
        if (existing) return { ..., duplicate: true };
      }
      throw err;
    }
    return { id, event_id: input.eventId ?? null, sequence, created_at: now, occurred_at: occurredAt, duplicate: false };
  }

  recordEvent(data: RecordEventInput): RecordedEvent {  // legacy — keep return shape {id, sequence, created_at}
    const r = this.append({ projectId, taskId, actor, source: 'core', eventType, payload });

  recordProgressEvent / recordVerificationEvent / recordRecoveryEvent — same shape

  listEvents(filter: ListEventsFilter) {
    // scope: taskId OR projectId(project-level, task_id IS NULL)
    let q = 'SELECT * FROM events WHERE '; const params = [];
    if (filter.taskId) { q += 'task_id = ?'; params.push(filter.taskId); }
    else if (filter.projectId) { q += 'project_id = ? AND task_id IS NULL'; params.push(filter.projectId); }

    if (filter.category) { q += ' AND category = ?'; params.push(...) }
    if (filter.source) { q += ' AND source = ?'; params.push(...) }
    q += ' ORDER BY sequence ASC, id ASC LIMIT ? OFFSET ?';
    ...
  }

  getEventsSince(taskId, sinceSequence) — unchanged

  private nextSequence(projectId, taskId) {
    const row = taskId
      ? this.db.raw.prepare('SELECT MAX(sequence) AS max_seq FROM events WHERE task_id = ?').get(taskId)
      : this.db.raw.prepare('SELECT MAX(sequence) AS max_seq FROM events WHERE project_id = ? AND task_id IS NULL').get(projectId);
    return ((row as any)?.max_seq || 0) + 1;
  }

  private findByEventId(eventId) { ... }
}

### 6. core domain/session-binding-service.ts

```ts
export class SessionBindingService {
  constructor(private db: Database) {}
  bind(sessionId, projectId, taskId, agentId) {
    const now = new Date().toISOString();
    // one ACTIVE binding per session: release the previous active one first
    this.db.raw.prepare('UPDATE session_task_bindings SET released_at = ? WHERE session_id = ? AND released_at IS NULL').run(now, sessionId);
    this.db.raw.prepare('INSERT INTO session_task_bindings (session_id, project_id, task_id, agent_id, created_at, released_at) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,NULL)').run(...);
  }
  releaseForTask(taskId) { UPDATE ... SET released_at = now WHERE task_id = ? AND released_at IS NULL }
  resolveTask(sessionId): {taskId, projectId} | null  — active binding
}
```

Per plan §10.5 (domain/ingest-service.ts):
```ts
export interface IngestEnvelope { event_id, type, source, actor, task_id?, occurred_at?, payload }
export interface IngestOutcome { results: [], accepted, duplicates, rejected }

export class IngestService {
  constructor(private db: Database) {}
  ingest(envelopes: IngestEnvelope[]): IngestOutcome {
    // single transaction over the batch
    this.db.raw.exec('BEGIN');
    try {
      for each env:
        if (!isIngestibleType(env.type)) → this should have been caught at route level with 400; keep guard → rejected (defensive)
        try {
          const taskId = env.task_id ?? this.resolveBindingTask(env) ?? null;
          const r = events.append({projectId, taskId, actor: env.actor, source: env.source, eventType: env.type, payload: env.payload, eventId: env.event_id, occurredAt: env.occurred_at});
          results.push(r.duplicate ? duplicate : accepted);
 if (accepted && env.type === 'progress_fulfilment') this.onFulfilment?.(...);
        } catch (err) { results.push({status:'rejected', reason: err.message}) }  // payload validation failure etc.
      COMMIT
    } catch { ROLLBACK; throw }
  }
}
```

### 8. core http/routes/ingest.ts

```ts
import type { FastifyInstance } from 'fastify';
import { ingestBatchSchema, ... , ValidationError, NotFoundError } from '@workjournal/shared';
import { validateBody } from '../hooks/validation.js';
import { IngestService } from '../../domain/ingest-service.js';

export async function registerIngestRoutes(server: FastifyInstance) {
  server.post('/events', { preHandler: [validateBody(ingestBatchSchema)] }, async (request) => {
    const project_id = (request.query as any).project_id; // also validate: string required
    if (!project_id) throw new ValidationError('project_id query parameter is required');
    const db = server.projectDbs.forProject(project_id);  // 400/404 via NotFoundError
    const body = request.body as {events: IngestEnvelopeInput[]};
    // ledger-only:400 if any envelope type not ingestible (D-023)
    const offending = body.events.filter(e => !isIngestibleType(e.type)).map(e => e.type);
    if (offending.length) throw new ValidationError(`Ledger-only ingestion: these event types are not ingestible: ${[...new Set(offending)].join(', ')}. Task lifecycle and transition events are recorded by Core only.`);
    return new IngestService(db).ingest(body.events, project_id);
  });
}
```

Wait — NotFoundError for unknown project: forProject throws NotFoundError (404?) — error handler: statusCode ||400. NotFoundError presumably has statusCode 404. Fine.

### 9. events route (WS-3.1)

```ts
export async function registerEventRoutes(server: FastifyInstance) {
  server.get('/', { preHandler: [validateQuery(listEventsQuerySchema)] }, async (request) => {
    const query = request.query as ListEventsQuery;
    if (!query.task_id && !query.project_id) throw new ValidationError('Provide exactly one of task_id or project_id');
    const scope = query.task_id ? server.projectDbs.forTask(query.task_id) : { db: server.projectDbs.forProject(query.project_id!) };
    return buildServices(scope.db).events.listEvents({...});
  });
}
```
Note current code uses forTask(query.task_id) → returns {db}. forProject returns db directly (not scope!). Adjust accordingly.

the\n-- events -> tasks FK is carried over to the rebuilt table.\n\nCREATE TABLE events_rebuilt (\n  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  project_id TEXT NOT NULL,\n  task_id TEXT REFERENCES tasks(id),\n  sequence INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  actor TEXT NOT NULL,\n  event_type TEXT NOT NULL,\n  category TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'task',\n  source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'core',\n  payload TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',\n  event_id TEXT,\n  occurred_at TEXT NOT NULL,\n  created_at TEXT NOT NULL\n);\n\nINSERT INTO events_rebuilt (id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, category, source, payload, event_id, occurred_at, created_at)\n  SELECT id, project_id, task_id, sequence, actor, event_type, 'task', 'core', payload, NULL, created_at, created_at\n  FROM events;\n\nDROP TABLE events;\nALTER TABLE events_rebuilt RENAME TO events;\n\n-- Per-task timeline ordering (pre-002 name kept).\nCREATE INDEX idx_events_task ON events(task_id, sequence);\n-- Project-level (task-less) history lookup.\nCREATE INDEX idx_events_project ON events(project_id);\n-- Plan §3.1: supports both query shapes (task timeline and project history).\nCREATE INDEX idx_events_project_task_seq ON events(project_id, task_id, sequence);\n-- Plan §3.2: category-filtered per-task queries.\nCREATE INDEX idx_events_task_category ON events(task_id, category, sequence);\n-- Idempotent ingestion dedup (D-023): one row per client event_id.\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_events_event_id ON events(event_id) WHERE event_id IS NOT NULL;\n"
}
```
